### 3.4 The five check steps, the validating state and the server's re-check

**The order (BQ-5 RULED; Condition 1).** A picked or dropped file is checked in a worker, cheapest step first, and only a file that passes every step reaches the preview (E15-S07's order, `BL:1136`; `14:633`): size → parse depth / node count → schema → version → per-pane registry → preview. The first step that fails ends the check: its rejection takes the progress's place, the later steps never run, and nothing of the file is kept (§5).

| #   | Step                     | What the worker checks                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | The limit (the Fixture paragraph)                                                                                                                                                                                            | When it fails, or what it finds                                                                                                                                                  | `layout.import_rejected{reason}`                       |
| --- | ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| 1   | size                     | the file's type and size, from its metadata, before a byte of it reaches the parser: a file over the cap is never parsed (`BL:1136`), and a picked file's type is checked too, because `accept` only filters what the OS picker offers                  | 2 <!-- audit:contract.size_mb=2 --> MB, E15-S07's default (`BL:1136`), PROPOSED for `22` (D09-G5)                                                                                                                            | `SCR-026/rejected-oversized`; a dropped file that is not JSON: `SCR-026/rejected-wrong-type`                                                                                     | `oversized`; `wrong-type`                              |
| 2   | parse depth / node count | the JSON, parsed in the worker with both limits counted while it parses, so a hostile file stalls a worker and never the page (`BL:1136`)                                                                                                               | depth 16 <!-- audit:contract.depth=16 --> levels, the server's own rule for a request body (`22:19626`); 10,000 <!-- audit:contract.nodes=10000 --> values (`BL:1136`), PROPOSED (D09-G5)                                    | `SCR-026/rejected-nodes` (the depth, with the node count its variant); invalid JSON: `SCR-026/rejected-malformed`'s parse variant, which names the line                          | `malformed`                                            |
| 3   | schema                   | the whole document against `WorkspaceBundle` (`22:21209–21230`): every required key present, a pane's kind an open string for a reader (`22:19778`), no layout over its panes, no workspace over its layouts, the indicator presets whole or not at all | 16 <!-- audit:contract.panes_max=16 --> panes a layout (`D02:133`); 32 <!-- audit:contract.layouts_max=32 --> layouts a workspace (`22:8725`); 200 <!-- audit:contract.presets_max=200 --> presets a file (`22:21229–21230`) | `SCR-026/rejected-malformed`: the missing key named in mono, or its panes, layouts and presets variants                                                                          | `malformed`                                            |
| 4   | version                  | the file's `schema_version` (`22:21215`) against the format version this app reads (BQ-4 RULED): newer is refused; older and still readable is migrated forward in the worker; older than the oldest this app reads is refused                          | this app reads format version 1 <!-- audit:fixture.app_format=1 -->; the order of versions and the oldest one read are D09-G2's                                                                                              | `SCR-026/rejected-version-newer`; `SCR-026/rejected-version-older`; a migration is no failure: `SCR-026/preview-migrated`                                                        | `version-newer`; `version-older`                       |
| 5   | per-pane registry        | each pane's kind against this build's registry (`22:19778`) and each pane setting against the registry's list for its kind (`BL:1136`)                                                                                                                  | —                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | nothing fails here: an unknown kind is a Drop row, never a refusal (AC 3); a setting the registry marks account-scoped is a Drop row; the Positions pane is the one Remap (§4.2) | —                                                      |
| —   | preview                  | the table of §4.2, built from the document already parsed, with no second parse (`BL:1136`)                                                                                                                                                             | the panel budget of the layout that opens (§4.4)                                                                                                                                                                             | over the budget: the budget line and the stacked confirm (§4.4)                                                                                                                  | `budget-exceeded`, only when that confirm is cancelled |

**The validating state (DL-11; BQ-9 RULED).** `SCR-026/validating` is CMP-023, determinate (`15:262–268`): its value moves a fifth at each step, and its value text, always shown, reads "Checking scalping.json… step 3 of 5" (`e15.import.checking`: `D02:515`'s words with the step added), the file's name capped at 40 <!-- audit:design.file_cap=40 --> characters as text. The bar sits under the file line, full body width; its track `color.surface.hover`, its fill `color.action.primary.default`, its value text `type.num.sm` + `color.text.secondary` (CMP-023's own tokens). A show-delay of 150 ms (BQ-9's default, ruled as proposed) keeps a file that validates within the 500 <!-- audit:contract.parse_ms=500 --> ms of `14:633` from flashing the bar; a check that ends inside the delay shows its outcome at once, with no start announcement. The step names stay in this spec and in the analytics mapping and are not printed: "schema" and "registry" are not a trader's words, so the value text counts the steps instead. That departs from `D02:515`, which names them, and is a PROPOSED C-row (D09-C13).

**What is spoken (BQ-13 RULED; `05:144`, `05:150`).** The dialog mounts one polite region, empty, with its body (`05:150`); CMP-023 is never a live region itself (`15:262–268`). The check speaks at most twice: once when the bar first shows, its value text at step 1, and once at its end, the preview's caption on a pass (the migration line first on a migrated file; over the budget the caption carries the budget sentence itself), or, on a fail, the rejection's own CMP-027, which speaks for itself (§5). Never per step, never per percent. On a pass focus stays on Choose file…, so the next `Tab` reaches the table (`05:96`); on a fail it moves to Choose another file once the message has been announced (§5).

**What the check in the browser is, and is not (Condition 1).** It is a courtesy: it tells the user early and keeps a hostile file away from the page, and it never replaces the server's own check. The server re-checks size, depth, nodes, schema and version when the file is imported (E15-S07, `BL:1136`); it refuses unknown fields on its write paths (SR-040, `04:853`) and treats the browser as hostile (`04:54`), and the ticket's Security notes say the same of every client guard (`BL:242`). `SCR-026/validating` says so in its `server:` clause, which the tool gates (S14). A file the server refuses at the send is `SCR-026/refused-server` (§5.1) and never a partial import, because the import is one request that creates everything or nothing (D09-G1, §12). Until it has passed, the file is never used to build a component, open a window or send a request (`BL:1136`), and no import path takes a filesystem path (SR-097, `04:922`). The worker reads the file as plain data and builds the preview only from the fields `WorkspaceBundle` names, so an unknown key, `__proto__` and `constructor` included, is never read into the app; whether the client refuses such a key outright is D09-G4's, because the bundle is not a closed schema (`22:21209–21230`), and E15-S07's fuzz corpus covers both readings (`BL:1136`).

**Nothing is kept.** Cancel during the check stops the worker and releases the file and everything parsed from it; a refusal at any step does the same, and so does a new pick, which starts again from the first step. Nothing from the file is stored, sent or logged before Import as new workspace, and E15-S07 logs never carry file contents (`BL:1136`).

### 3.5 The send, the creating state and every frame of §3.1

**The send (BQ-9 RULED; D-30, `D02:148`).** Import as new workspace sends the checked file once, and the server creates the new workspace with all of it in one request, or nothing (D09-G1, §12: no operation of `22` takes a bundle, `22:21226`, and E15-S07 lands one first, C-6.1; done in the client as `createWorkspace` and then `createLayout` per layout, the import would not be atomic, `FL:171`, which is why no frame here shows a partial state). Over the panel budget, the stacked confirm comes first (§4.4). A name already in use is the server's to settle, never a rename field: the preview's clash line names the result, "Imported as Scalping (2): a workspace named Scalping exists." (`D02:516`), and the report names the name the server gave (§4.5). The new workspace starts unpinned, and environment binding is never imported (the ticket's Security notes, `BL:242`).

**Creating (`SCR-026/creating`).** Import as new workspace keeps its label, with `aria-busy="true"` and CMP-001's spinner after the label, its width fixed (`15:48`; CMP-001's buy and sell keep their label the same way, PROPOSED for this primary so the effect stays named while it runs). Cancel becomes Close, because a sent file can no longer be called back. The zone and Choose file… leave the body, so nothing can be chosen mid-send, an absence of the step and not a lock; the table stays, read-only and still scrollable. Close, `Esc`, the × and a press on the scrim close the view only: the import goes on and finishes (`15:468`; H22, `S4:734`). After CMP-001's slow-path delay, "Still importing…" shows under the footer, end-aligned in `type.ui.caption` + `color.text.secondary`, and is spoken once, politely (PROPOSED copy, `e15.import.slow`). Focus stays on Import as new workspace.

**When the view was closed during the send (PROPOSED; H22's outcome surface, `S4:734`).** The new workspace still opens in a tab, and a persistent CMP-045 `success` toast writes the report's success line (`e15.import.report.done`) once into CMP-212's polite region, with the action View report (`e15.import.report.view`), which reopens SCR-026 at its report (§4.5); a toast with an action stays until it is dismissed (`15:483`). A refusal that arrives after the view closed raises a persistent CMP-045 `danger` toast, "Nothing was imported." (`e15.import.nothing`), with the action Review (`e15.import.review`), which reopens SCR-026 at the refusal's frame (§5.1). Neither toast is the report: the report is the dialog's region (BQ-12 RULED).

**When the session changes while the dialog is open (`SCR-026/import-leg-lost`; §6).** If the session loses `workspaces:write` (`D02:645`), Import as new workspace turns T2 where it stands, with `rbac.permission`'s reason under the button (2a). A view-as context that starts meanwhile does the same through the SCR-124 banner, the one notice. Choose file…, the zone, the check and the table are client work and stay T1 for every session. A workspace limit the client has not learned is the send's refusal, `SCR-026/refused-limit` (§5.1). The rule, its anatomy and the order of the legs are §6's alone.

**Cancelled (`SCR-026/cancelled`; `FL:162`).** Cancel, `Esc`, the × or a press on the scrim before the send closes the dialog at once, with no discard prompt, because a chosen file is not unsaved work (H36, `S4:904`): the worker stops, the file and everything parsed from it are released, no event fires and nothing is announced, and focus returns to the menu's button.

**Density and zoom.** `SCR-026/compact` changes two values: the controls take `size.control.compact`, 24 <!-- audit:redline.size.control.compact=24 --> px, and the table's rows take the compact `size.row.height`, 24 <!-- audit:redline.size.row.height=24 --> px; padding, gaps and type are unchanged (`16` §3). `SCR-026/reflow-200` is CMP-043's reflow (`15:463–468`; `05:131`): the dialog fills the viewport, the header and the footer stay in view and the footer wraps in its order, the body scrolls, and the table stacks one row element per record (`15:529`), each record its Item, Type, Action and Reason as labelled lines with the Action's glyph beside its word.

**Per frame: what changes, the components and the focus.** Each row names a frame of §3.1 by its state; the anatomy of §3.2 and §3.3 holds unless the row says otherwise.

| State              | What changes (layout, components and variants, tokens)                                                                                                              | Focus order, keys and announcements                                                                                                                                                                                          |

| entry-view-as      | the item in §6's form, pointing at the SCR-124 banner, the one notice, with no reason line of its own (`D02:635–674`)                                               | the arrow keys reach it; `Enter` or `Space` opens nothing and re-shows the reason once, politely (§6); `Esc` closes the menu and returns focus to its button                                                                 |

| pick               | §3.2's chrome: the trust note, the zone with Choose file… and its words, the help line; the footer Cancel; CMP-043, CMP-002, CMP-028, CMP-067, CMP-001 ×2           | opens on Choose file…; `Tab`: Choose file… → Cancel → × → Choose file…; `Enter` or `Space` on Choose file… opens the OS picker; on open only the title and the trust note are read                                           |
| drag-over          | the zone's accept state (§3.3); nothing else moves                                                                                                                  | focus stays where it was; nothing is announced                                                                                                                                                                               |
| drag-over-rejected | the zone's reject state (§3.3)                                                                                                                                      | focus stays where it was; a drop of two files speaks its words once, politely (§3.3)                                                                                                                                         |
| validating         | the file line and CMP-023 under the zone (§3.4); Choose file… stays live                                                                                            | focus stays on Choose file…; one polite announcement at the start and one at the end (§3.4); Cancel stops the worker and closes the dialog                                                                                   |
| creating           | Import as new workspace busy, its label kept; Cancel becomes Close; the zone leaves; the table read-only; the slow-path line under the footer after CMP-001's delay | focus stays on Import as new workspace; `Tab`: the table's scroll region → Close → Import as new workspace → × → the scroll region; Close, `Esc`, the × and the scrim close the view only; the slow-path line once, politely |
| import-leg-lost    | Import as new workspace in §6's form where it stands, its reason "Your role can't save workspaces." under it (2a); nothing else changes                             | focus stays where it was; one announcement (§6)                                                                                                                                                                              |
| cancelled          | no dialog                                                                                                                                                           | focus on the menu's button; nothing is announced                                                                                                                                                                             |
| compact            | the controls at `size.control.compact` and the rows at the compact `size.row.height`; nothing else                                                                  | as pick                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| reflow-200         | the dialog fills the viewport; the header and the footer stay in view; the body scrolls; the table stacks (`15:529`)                                                | as at 100 %; nothing is clipped (`05:131`)                                                                                                                                                                                   |

## 4. The preview table and the report

The preview is the whole design (the ticket's Technical notes, `BL:242`): an import that only asks whether to go ahead asks the user to trust an opaque file, while a table that lists every drop and remap first, and every addition after, makes the import an informed decision. One table serves both moments, the preview before the send (§4.2) and the report after it (§4.3), row for row (AC 3, AC 4). The frames below are binding and the tool gates them row by row, and §4.2 and §4.3 are the fixture's rows, gated cell by cell; §4.4 gives the preview's anatomy, the budget line, the stacked confirm and the migration line, and §4.5 the report with Copy report.

### 4.4 The preview: the table, the budget line, the stacked confirm and the migration line

**The table (BQ-10 RULED; DL-7, DL-8; `14:632`).** CMP-049 in table mode (`15:524–530`): a semantic `<table>` whose `<caption>` comes first, so a screen reader hears the counts before any row (the ticket's Accessibility notes), then a header row of four `<th scope="col">`, Item · Type · Action · Reason, then one row per element with its Item as the row header (`<th scope="row">`), so every cell is read with its column and its item. It is static: no control in a body row, no sort, no select column and no per-row skip (SCR-089's per-row choice, `E36D03:153–155`, is not this surface's), so it stays a table and never becomes a grid (`15:524–530`). Its one Tab stop is the scroll region around it, named by the caption (`15:524–530`): the arrow keys scroll it, and a screen reader's table commands move cell by cell with the headers read (AC 6). Every row stays in the page, never windowed, so table navigation and the browser's find reach all of them; a file holds at most 32 <!-- audit:contract.layouts_max=32 --> layouts of 16 <!-- audit:contract.panes_max=16 --> panes each (PROPOSED: CMP-049's `virtualized` off here). It is never a canvas and never a picture (`14:632`; `D02:516`).

**The order (DL-8; the ticket's Technical notes).** Drops and remaps first and additions after, so the surprising rows lead: the Drop rows (the unknown kinds, then the dropped settings, each in the file's order), then the Remap rows, then the Add rows (the layouts, then the panels in their layouts' order, then one summary row each for symbols, drawings, chart templates and indicator presets). §4.2 is the fixture's 16 <!-- audit:fixture.rows=16 --> rows in that order, which the tool checks row by row.

**The caption (DL-19).** It leads with the counts the user is deciding on, "Preview — 7 panels and 2 layouts to add · 3 dropped · 1 remapped" (`e15.import.caption`), each count derived by the tool from the rows: 7 <!-- audit:fixture.panels=7 --> panels, 2 <!-- audit:fixture.layouts=2 --> layouts, 3 <!-- audit:fixture.dropped=3 --> dropped (2 <!-- audit:fixture.unknown_kinds=2 --> of them unknown kinds) and 1 <!-- audit:fixture.remapped=1 --> remapped. A remapped panel counts among the panels it adds, and a dropped item is never counted as added. The caption is `type.ui.label` + `color.text.secondary`, above the header row (`15:524–531`); over the budget its last sentence names the layout that opens and its estimate (below).

**The Action column (BQ-10 RULED; DoD-4).** Exactly three words, each after a decorative CMP-019 glyph in its own cell (`aria-hidden`, `15:225`): Add (`check`), Drop (`x-circle`, the PROPOSED glyph `15:307` registers) and Remap (`merge`, PROPOSED). The word carries the meaning, the glyph's shape repeats it, and colour is a third cue, never the only one. The word is `type.ui.body-strong` in `color.text.primary` on every row; only the glyph, at `size.icon` 16 <!-- audit:redline.size.icon=16 --> px, takes a status colour: Add `color.status.success.strong`, Drop `color.status.danger.strong`, Remap `color.status.info.strong`, each measured as non-text on both stripes in §8. Remap takes the info hue rather than the warning one because in light `color.status.danger.strong` and `color.status.warning.strong` resolve to the same amber, so Drop and Remap would share a colour; under tritanopia the Add and Remap colours still come close (§8's colour-vision rows), which the two words and the two shapes cover.

**The Item, Type and Reason columns.** Item is the element as the file names it, a stored kind name such as `heatmap_v2`, a layout's name, a panel's symbol, interval and sync group, or a summary such as "2 symbols: BTCUSDT, ETHUSDT", set in `type.num.sm` + `color.text.primary` down the whole column, so a value from the file never changes typeface mid-column and always reads as data. Type is the app's word for the element (the registry's kind for a known panel; "panel" for a kind this build does not know, AC 3; "setting" for a dropped pane setting), in `type.ui.body` + `color.text.primary`. Reason is the app's own sentence in `type.ui.body` + `color.text.secondary`, and an em dash on an Add row. The unknown kinds keep their stored names and the ticket's reason, "not available in this version" (AC 3); `D02:516`'s "not known to this version" is a C-row (D09-C6). Item takes the width the other three columns leave; Type and Action fit their longest word; Reason wraps, and its row grows with it.

**Account-scoped elements (BQ-3 RULED; AC 4).** An account filter on a pane is dropped and listed, never remapped: its row reads Account filter on Positions · setting · Drop · "workspaces never carry account bindings" (`D02:516`; `11:2868`), and nothing in the preview, the report, the copied text, an announcement or an event names the account it pointed at, or any account the importer cannot see. The value of a dropped setting is never shown, only where it was. The one Remap is the Positions pane itself, which shows your own accounts once it is imported; its Reason names no account at all, because what changes is the pane's scope and not a stored binding. The drop is the client's reading of the file; the server drops such a setting again at import (`BL:1136`), and until `22` says how (D09-G3), a strict server may refuse the whole file instead, which is `SCR-026/refused-server` (§5.1) and never a partial import. `14:628–629`'s "remap to the user's default account" gets a PROPOSED A-row (§11).

**Strings from the file (Condition 1; DL-17).** Every string the table takes from the file (kind names, layout names, symbols, intervals, drawing labels) is a text node and nothing else: never markup, never a link, never interpreted (SR-127, `04:965`; C-12.10). Each is capped before it reaches the page, at names 40 <!-- audit:contract.name_max=40 -->, symbols 20 <!-- audit:contract.symbol_max=20 -->, panel kinds 32 <!-- audit:contract.kind_max=32 -->, keys 64 <!-- audit:design.key_cap=64 -->, file names 40 <!-- audit:design.file_cap=40 --> and drawing labels 120 <!-- audit:contract.label_max=120 --> characters (the Fixture paragraph): a longer value is cut at its cap and ends in "…", so nothing past a cap is rendered, announced or copied. Within its cap, a value wider than its column ends in an ellipsis on screen and keeps the whole capped value in the cell's text, for a screen reader and for Copy report. Each value is direction-isolated (`<bdi>`), and each control or bidirectional-formatting character in it (the set `22:19626–19631` makes the server strip from a name) shows as a visible "�" instead of acting, so no file string can reorder the row around it or hide part of itself (PROPOSED).

**Striping, the header and the rows (PROPOSED A-row to CMP-049, §10).** Inside a dialog the table sits on `color.surface.overlay`, where CMP-049's panel stripes (odd `color.surface.canvas`, `15:524–531`) would read as holes, so the odd rows take `color.surface.overlay` and the even rows `color.surface.raised`. The header row is `color.surface.hover` with `type.ui.label` + `color.text.primary` and a `border.hairline` 1 <!-- audit:redline.border.hairline=1 --> px `color.border.subtle` rule under it, sticky at the top of the scroll region (`15:524–530`). The rows are the density's `size.row.height` (24 <!-- audit:redline.size.row.height=24 --> px compact) and grow when a Reason wraps; each cell is padded `space.inset.sm` 8 <!-- audit:redline.space.inset.sm=8 --> px. The table's region takes the height the blocks above it leave inside CMP-043's cap (`15:466`), so the footer never scrolls away, and the focused region carries the dialog's ring inside its edge.

**The lines above the table.** In the order a reader meets them: the trust note (§3.2); the zone and the file line (§3.3); at most one CMP-028 line, the migration line or the budget line, `live="off"` because the end of the check speaks it (§3.4); then the clash line when a workspace already has the file's name, "Imported as Scalping (2): a workspace named Scalping exists." (`D02:516`; `e15.import.clash`), in `type.ui.body` + `color.text.secondary`, the server's disambiguated name as the client predicts it (`BL:1136`); then the table. Each CMP-028 line is full width with a `border.thick` 2 <!-- audit:redline.border.thick=2 --> px start edge, padding `space.inset.sm` 8 <!-- audit:redline.space.inset.sm=8 --> px and `radius.card` 6 <!-- audit:redline.radius.card=6 --> px; the migration line's words are `color.text.primary` on `color.status.info.subtle`, the budget line's on `color.status.warning.subtle`, and each edge and glyph takes its tone's `.strong` (§8).

**The panel budget (BQ-6 RULED; DL-9, DL-16; the ticket's Technical notes and Performance notes).** The check is per layout, for the layout that opens, which is the file's default layout or else its first: one layout is mounted at a time, so a sum over the whole file would warn about panels that are never on screen together. Its estimate is E15-K01's per-kind cost model over that layout's panels against the frame time measured now (D-15, `D02:133`; `D02:292`), held to the 16 <!-- audit:contract.budget_ms=16 --> ms p95 budget (C-14.2). Under the budget nothing is shown and Import as new workspace sends at once. Over it, the preview says so twice before anything is sent: the caption's last sentence (`e15.import.caption_budget`) and the CMP-028 `warning` line above the table (`e15.import.budget`). The fixture `wall.json` opens Wall with 12 <!-- audit:fixture.budget_panels=12 --> panels: 11.8 <!-- audit:fixture.measured_ms=11.8 --> ms per frame measured now and about 17.9 <!-- audit:fixture.estimate_ms=17.9 --> ms estimated. A file with more panes in one layout than the 16 <!-- audit:contract.panes_max=16 --> a layout holds never reaches the budget: it is a schema rejection that names the limit, the panes variant of `SCR-026/rejected-malformed` (the ticket's 30-panel fixture; more than 16 cannot occur for a stored layout, `D02:292`).

**The stacked confirm (`SCR-026/budget-confirm`; BQ-6 RULED; DL-16).** Over the budget, Import as new workspace opens US-LAY-001's confirm, the CMP-044 E15-D03 drew for Add panel (`D03:629–631`), with the import's verb and never a second warning: a `role="alertdialog"` stacked over SCR-026 with its own scrim, at most 560 <!-- audit:contract.dialog_md_px=560 --> px wide (`15:466`), titled "Open Wall with 12 panels?" (`layout.cost.import.title`), its body the cost sentence with the measured and the estimated frame time and their fps (`layout.cost.import.body`, `D03:630`'s sentence with the import's clause), and its footer Cancel (`secondary`, focused) · Import anyway (`primary`). The three `layout.cost.import.*` strings are PROPOSED, an A-row to E15-D03's deck (D09-A20). Import anyway is a CMP-001 `primary` and not `danger`, because nothing is destroyed and the stutter it accepts ends when panels close; it takes no activation in CMP-044's first `confirmDelayMs` and no key repeat (`15:473–481`). Cancel and `Esc` return to the preview with focus on Import as new workspace and fire `layout.import_rejected{reason: budget-exceeded}` once: the ticket's sixth reason counts a user who declined the stutter, not a refused file (BQ-6 RULED). A press on the confirm's scrim leaves it open, as for any `alertdialog` (`15:463–468`). Import anyway sends the file (§3.5).

**The migration line (BQ-4 RULED; `BL:1136`).** A file whose `schema_version` is older than the format version this app reads, and which E15-S07's migration can carry forward, is migrated in the worker before the preview, and the preview says so above the table: "Migrated from format version 1 to 2." (`e15.import.migrated`; CMP-028 `info`). The table then lists what the migrated file creates, and the report of a migrated import carries the same line above its table (PROPOSED; not drawn). The comparison reads `schema_version` alone, never an app version (`22:21215`); the order of versions and the oldest one still read are D09-G2's to fix in `22`. A newer file, or an older one past that oldest, is refused (§5).

**Per frame: what changes, the components and the focus.**

| State               | What changes (layout, components and variants, tokens)                                                                                                                                                                            | Focus order, keys and announcements                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| preview             | the zone and the file line stay; the clash line; CMP-049 with §4.2's rows in its scroll region; the footer Cancel · Import as new workspace; CMP-043, CMP-002, CMP-028, CMP-067, CMP-049, CMP-019 ×3, CMP-001 ×3                  | focus stays on Choose file…; `Tab`: Choose file… → the table's scroll region → Cancel → Import as new workspace → × → Choose file…; the end of the check speaks the caption once, politely                                                     |
| preview-migrated    | as the preview, with the migration line above the table and no clash line                                                                                                                                                         | as the preview; the end of the check speaks the migration line, then the caption                                                                                                                                                               |
| preview-over-budget | as the preview, with the budget line above the table and the budget sentence closing the caption; the table is `wall.json`'s                                                                                                      | as the preview; the caption, budget sentence included, is spoken once; Import as new workspace opens the stacked confirm                                                                                                                       |
| budget-confirm      | a CMP-044 `standard`, `md`, stacked over SCR-026 with its own scrim; its title `type.ui.heading-sm` + `color.text.primary`, its body `type.ui.body` + `color.text.secondary`, the numbers in the body's type; the footer as above | opens on Cancel; `Tab`: Cancel → Import anyway → Cancel; `Esc` is Cancel; a press on its scrim leaves it open; the title and the body are read as it opens (an `alertdialog`, `05:96`); after Cancel, focus is back on Import as new workspace |

### 4.5 The report and Copy report

**The report (BQ-12 RULED; DL-10; AC 3, AC 4).** When the import succeeds, the new workspace opens in a tab behind the dialog (`D02:520`) and the dialog's body becomes its last step: a `role="region"` named "Import report" by its heading, an `h3` with `tabindex="-1"` that takes focus on arrival, a move the user's own Import as new workspace made, announced as focus lands (`05:96`). Inside the region, in order: the CMP-028 `success` line "Imported Scalping (2): 7 panels, 2 layouts, 3 items dropped, 1 remapped." (`e15.import.report.done`, `D02:520`'s line with this fixture's counts and the remap count added), spoken once, politely, and naming the name the server gave; then the same CMP-049 table re-rendered with what was applied, dropped and remapped, row for row as §4.3, its caption "Report — 7 panels and 2 layouts added · 3 dropped · 1 remapped" (`e15.import.report.caption`). The unknown kinds are repeated by their stored names (AC 3's second leg); the account filter is listed as dropped; the Positions pane is the one Remap; and each says so in plain words that name no account ("workspaces never carry account bindings", "shows your own accounts"; AC 4). The trust note and the zone are gone from this step, because the file has been used.

**It persists (BQ-12 RULED).** The report stays until Close: no timer, never a toast (CMP-045) and never `role="status"`; every line, the caption and every cell are selectable text. Close, `Esc`, the × and a press on the scrim end it, and focus lands on the new workspace's tab, the host's named fallback, since Import… and its menu are gone (`15:463–468`). Once closed, the report is gone from the app; `layout.imported` is the record that remains, analytics and never audit (`14:634`; `D02:84`).

**Copy report (CMP-033; BQ-12 RULED; DL-10).** Copy report is CMP-033's behaviour in a labelled footer button, a CMP-001 `secondary` (CMP-033 is an icon button today, `15:361`; its labelled form is a PROPOSED A-row, §10). It puts the report on the clipboard as plain text, in the region's order: the success line, the caption, then one line per row, the row's number first and its four cells after it, joined by " · " ("1 · heatmap_v2 · panel · Drop · not available in this version"). No copied line starts with a value from the file, so a spreadsheet the text is pasted into never reads one as a formula, and a control character from the file arrives as the "�" the table shows, never as a tab or a line break (PROPOSED). The values are the capped ones (Condition 1), and nothing else of the file travels. It announces "Copied" once through CMP-033's own polite region and never speaks the copied text (`15:361–367`); its `check` glyph shows CMP-033's copied state, and focus stays on it. If the browser refuses the clipboard, "Couldn't copy the report." is spoken once, politely, and the region stays selectable for a copy by hand (PROPOSED copy, `e15.import.report.copy_failed`).

**The event (DL-20).** `layout.imported{panelCount, droppedCount, remappedCount, schemaVersion}` fires once, when the server has created the workspace, and never on the press (`14:634`): 7 <!-- audit:fixture.panels=7 -->, 3 <!-- audit:fixture.dropped=3 --> and 1 <!-- audit:fixture.remapped=1 --> for the fixture, with `schemaVersion` the file's own `schema_version`, as read before any migration, so a migrated import stays visible. E15-S07's `{panes, dropped, migratedFrom}` names the same facts; the alias table is D09-G13 (§12).

| Part         | Component and variant                                                              | Layout and redlines                                                                                                                                                                                                | Tokens                                                                                                                        |
| ------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Region       | `<section role="region">`, `aria-labelledby` its heading                           | the whole body: the heading, the success line `space.stack.md` 12 <!-- audit:redline.space.stack.md=12 --> px under it, the table under that                                                                       | —                                                                                                                             |
| Heading      | an `h3`, "Import report", `tabindex="-1"`                                          | one line; the dialog's focus ring when it holds focus                                                                                                                                                              | `type.ui.body-strong` + `color.text.primary`                                                                                  |
| Success line | CMP-028 `success`, `live="polite"`                                                 | full width; a `border.thick` 2 <!-- audit:redline.border.thick=2 --> px start edge; padding `space.inset.sm` 8 <!-- audit:redline.space.inset.sm=8 --> px; `radius.card` 6 <!-- audit:redline.radius.card=6 --> px | fill `color.status.success.subtle`; edge and glyph `color.status.success.strong`; words `type.ui.body` + `color.text.primary` |
| Table        | CMP-049 table mode, as the preview's (§4.4)                                        | the height the heading and the line leave; its own scroll region, named by its caption                                                                                                                             | as §4.4                                                                                                                       |
| Footer       | Copy report (CMP-001 `secondary`, CMP-033's behaviour) · Close (CMP-001 `primary`) | as §3.2's footer                                                                                                                                                                                                   | as §3.2's footer                                                                                                              |

**Per frame (`SCR-026/report`).** What changes: the body is the region above, the trust note and the zone gone; CMP-043, CMP-002, CMP-028, CMP-049, CMP-019 ×3, CMP-001 ×2 with CMP-033's behaviour on Copy report. Focus, keys and announcements: focus on the heading; `Tab`: the table's scroll region → Copy report → Close → × → the scroll region; `Enter` or `Space` on Copy report copies; Close, `Esc`, the × and the scrim close the dialog and focus lands on the new workspace's tab; the success line is spoken once, politely, and "Copied" once per copy.

**Copy this section adds (PROPOSED).** Four strings that no frame row keys, each one sentence or one verb, are rows of the deck (§9): `e15.import.slow`, `e15.import.report.view`, `e15.import.review` and `e15.import.report.copy_failed`.

## 5. Rejections and refusals

**Every rejection and every refusal here is an outcome — never a lock, never a partial import** (BQ-4, BQ-5, BQ-11 RULED; Conditions 1 and 2; A1). Six frames refuse a file before anything is sent: the newer and the older format version, the malformed file, the oversized file, the wrong type and the parse limits. Two refuse it at the send: the workspace limit and the server's own re-check (§1.3, states 15–21, 23 and 24). Each is one CMP-027 `inline` message with `live="polite"` in the import dialog's body, above the still-live Choose file…, so the dialog stays open and another file is chosen without starting over (AC 5; `D02:517`; `D02:518`). The footer carries Cancel and, on every frame but the limit's, Choose another file, the primary, focused on arrival. No Import verb is drawn on any of them; it is omitted, never T2-locked (AC 2; `11:2871`; §6). Each says "Nothing was imported." and it is true: no workspace, layout or pane exists, and nothing of the file is kept (§5.5).

### 5.2 The rejection frames' anatomy

One anatomy serves the six rejections and the two refusals; only the message, its variants and the footer change (§5.1). Top to bottom inside the CMP-043 `size="lg"` card, at most 960 <!-- audit:contract.dialog_lg_px=960 --> px wide (`15:466`): the title row, "Import workspace" (`h2`) with the close × "Close Import workspace"; the trust note, CMP-028 `info` `role="note"`, unchanged from `SCR-026/pick`; the message; Choose file… with its words "or drop a workspace file here" and its help "JSON, up to 2 MB", still live, so a pick or a drop starts the five checks again from size (§5.4); the footer. Nothing else is drawn: no table, no progress, no list of what would have been added. Choose another file and Choose file… front the same native input: the footer's button is the constructive primary that focus lands on, so `Enter` reopens the picker at once, and the body's button keeps its drop words for a pointer. The message is CandleViewer's own words with at most one string from outside the app in it (§5.5), never a parser's or a server's text (`15:306`).

| Part                                    | Component and variant                                                                                                         | Tokens (one set of names; dark, light and high contrast resolve them, §8)                                                                                                                                                                    | Redlines (comfortable; compact changes only the control heights)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

### 5.3 The version rule (BQ-4 RULED, owner approval pending (#2701))

The file's `schema_version` is compared with the format versions this app reads, and nothing else is: never an app version, never the build or the date that exported the file (`22:21215`; E15-S07, `BL:1136`). The contract types it as a string with no order (`22:21215`), so this spec reads it as a whole number written in decimal and compares it as a number, PROPOSED — D09-G2 asks `22`'s owner for the format, the order and the oldest version still read (the floor). Today the app reads format version 1 <!-- audit:fixture.app_format=1 -->, which is both its newest and its floor; the newer fixture is format version 2 <!-- audit:fixture.newer_format=2 --> and the older one format version 0 <!-- audit:fixture.older_format=0 -->. The migrated row below is a later build's, one that reads format version 2 and still reads 1.

| The file's `schema_version`                                                                                                          | Outcome                                                                                                                                                                 | Frame                            | Words (§9)                                                      | Event                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| above the newest this app reads (the fixture: 2 <!-- audit:fixture.newer_format=2 --> against 1 <!-- audit:fixture.app_format=1 -->) | refused whole, both versions named, then the way on: export it again from an app that writes the older format, or update this app (AC 2; `11:2871`; `D02:517`)          | `SCR-026/rejected-version-newer` | `e15.import.rejected.version_newer`                             | `version-newer`                                                                          |
| the newest this app reads                                                                                                            | the checks go on to the registry, then the preview                                                                                                                      | `SCR-026/preview`                | —                                                               | —                                                                                        |
| older, still read (a later build, the file one format behind it)                                                                     | migrated forward before the preview, which shows the migrated document, and said so above the table: "Migrated from format version 1 to 2." (E15-S07's rule, `BL:1136`) | `SCR-026/preview-migrated`       | `e15.import.migrated`                                           | none at the preview; on success `layout.imported` carries the file's own `schemaVersion` |
| below the floor (the fixture: 0 <!-- audit:fixture.older_format=0 --> against 1 <!-- audit:fixture.app_format=1 -->)                 | refused whole, both versions named, then the way on: update the app that exported it, then export it again (the ticket's `version-older`)                               | `SCR-026/rejected-version-older` | `e15.import.rejected.version_older`                             | `version-older`                                                                          |
| absent, empty, or not a whole number                                                                                                 | a schema failure, the key `schema_version` named in mono: "is missing" when it is absent, "isn't allowed" otherwise (§5.4)                                              | `SCR-026/rejected-malformed`     | `e15.import.rejected.malformed` · `e15.import.rejected.invalid` | `malformed`                                                                              |

Both refusals name both numbers in the words the user reads — "format version 2", "format version 1" — never "v2", a build number or an app's name; the newer refusal's last sentence is the constructive route the ticket asks for, export again from the older version or update this app (`BL:266`), and the older refusal's points the other way, at the app that wrote the file. No variant offers to bring in part of the file, to skip what this build cannot read or to go on regardless: the verb is absent (AC 2). The numbers printed are the parsed whole numbers and the app's own constant, so the file's raw `schema_version` text never reaches the screen (§5.5). E15-D02's refusal said "version 2" and "version 1" (`D02:517`); "format version" names what is compared, and the ticket's "This layout was exported by v2 and can't be imported into v1." (`14:629`) is replaced by the ruled words (BQ-4 RULED; `14:629`'s A-row, §11).

### 5.4 The limits, rendered from the tool

Every number below is the tool's (`--json`, `numbers.contract`), printed with its marker; each frame prints the same number in its message and the tool compares it clause by clause. The checks run cheapest first, and the first that fails ends the run, so a 50 MB file is refused before it is read (BQ-5 RULED; E15-S07, `BL:1136`): size → parse depth / node count → schema → version → per-pane registry → preview. Nothing is created by any of them; only Import as new workspace sends, and only after the preview (Condition 1).

| Step       | Limit                                                                                                                  | Source                                                                                                                                                                                             | Past it                                                                                     | Words (§9)                       |
| ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| 1 size     | a file of at most 2 <!-- audit:contract.size_mb=2 --> MB, read from the chosen file's size before a byte of it is read | E15-S07's default (`BL:1136`) until `22` sets the cap (D09-G5); a 2 <!-- audit:contract.size_mb=2 --> MB file is checked within 500 <!-- audit:contract.parse_ms=500 --> ms in a worker (`14:633`) | `SCR-026/rejected-oversized`                                                                | `e15.import.rejected.oversized`  |
| 2 parse    | nesting at most 16 <!-- audit:contract.depth=16 --> levels                                                             | the server's own rule for a workspace body (`22:19626`), reused (BQ-5 RULED)                                                                                                                       | `SCR-026/rejected-nodes`                                                                    | `e15.import.rejected.depth`      |
| 2 parse    | at most 10,000 <!-- audit:contract.nodes=10000 --> values, PROPOSED                                                    | E15-S07 names a node-count limit and fuzzes "a document with 10000 nested nodes" (`BL:1136`); no contract carries a number (D09-G5)                                                                | `SCR-026/rejected-nodes`, its variant                                                       | `e15.import.rejected.nodes`      |
| 3 schema   | the bundle's shape: every required key, every type, the closed objects' keys (`22:21209–21230`)                        | `22:21209–21230`; unknown keys refused, never coerced (`04:853`; C-6.3); the bundle and `ChartTemplateInput` are not closed yet (D09-G4)                                                           | `SCR-026/rejected-malformed`                                                                | `e15.import.rejected.malformed`  |
| 3 schema   | at most 16 <!-- audit:contract.panes_max=16 --> panels a layout                                                        | the contract's 16 (`D02:133`)                                                                                                                                                                      | `SCR-026/rejected-malformed`, its variant                                                   | `e15.import.rejected.panes`      |
| 3 schema   | at most 32 <!-- audit:contract.layouts_max=32 --> layouts a workspace                                                  | the store's limit (`22:8725`); the bundle carries none (D09-G5)                                                                                                                                    | `SCR-026/rejected-malformed`, its variant                                                   | `e15.import.rejected.layouts`    |
| 3 schema   | at most 200 <!-- audit:contract.presets_max=200 --> indicator presets, the array whole or not at all                   | `22:21229–21230`, where the server also refuses the array beyond 1 MiB of preset JSON                                                                                                              | `SCR-026/rejected-malformed`, its variant; the server's refusal is `SCR-026/refused-server` | `e15.import.rejected.presets`    |
| 4 version  | the format versions this app reads (§5.3)                                                                              | `22:21215`; D09-G2                                                                                                                                                                                 | the two version refusals; the migrated preview                                              | §5.3                             |
| 5 registry | each pane's kind and each setting against the registry                                                                 | the open-ended kinds (`22:19778`); settings that never hold account ids (`22:19838`)                                                                                                               | never a rejection: an unknown kind or an account-scoped setting is a Drop row (§4.2)        | `e15.import.reason.unknown_kind` |
| the send   | at most 32 <!-- audit:contract.workspaces_max=32 --> workspaces a user                                                 | `22:8525`                                                                                                                                                                                          | `SCR-026/refused-limit`                                                                     | `e15.import.refused.limit`       |
| the send   | everything above, checked again by the server                                                                          | E15-S07 (`BL:1136`); `04:54`; C-2.12                                                                                                                                                               | `SCR-026/refused-server`                                                                    | `e15.import.refused.server`      |

**How the parse step stays bounded.** The worker reads the text once, counting the nesting and the values as it goes, and stops at the first limit it crosses, before any document is built; at 2 <!-- audit:contract.size_mb=2 --> MB that stays inside the parse budget of `14:633`, and a hostile file stalls the worker, never the dialog or the trading panes behind it (E15-S07's containment note, `BL:1136`). **How the schema step names a key.** It names the first failure in document order, one key per message (`14:632`): a required key that is missing takes `e15.import.rejected.malformed`'s words; a present key that is not allowed, or a value of the wrong kind, takes the same frame with the PROPOSED `e15.import.rejected.invalid`, "This isn't a workspace file: `{key}` isn't allowed. Nothing was imported." — the server's own phrasing for its re-check, and the frame's variant (§9). The duplicate keys and the prototype-shaped keys of E15-S07's fuzz corpus (`BL:1136`) are refused the same way and never reach an object.

**The budget is a confirm, not a refusal** (BQ-6 RULED). A file whose layout that opens would cost more than the frame budget is a valid file: the preview says so (`SCR-026/preview-over-budget`), and Import as new workspace asks through US-LAY-001's measured-cost confirm, re-verbed Import anyway (`SCR-026/budget-confirm`, §4; `D02:292`); its Cancel fires `layout.import_rejected{reason: budget-exceeded}` and returns to the preview, so `budget-exceeded`, the last of the ticket's six reasons, belongs to that confirm and never to a rejection frame. Past the hard cap it is a refusal: the ticket's "30-panel file" (its test plan, `BL:266`) is a layout over the 16 <!-- audit:contract.panes_max=16 --> panels a layout holds (`D02:133`), refused whole with `e15.import.rejected.panes`.

### 5.5 Untrusted strings, nothing kept, and the refusals at the send

**Condition 1, applied to a refusal** (RULED, binding on every frame). A refusal shows at most one string that came from outside the app: the offending key (`SCR-026/rejected-malformed`, `SCR-026/refused-server`) or the chosen file's name (`SCR-026/rejected-wrong-type`). Every other part of a message is the app's own: the size comes from the file's size before it is read, the line from the parser, the counts from counting, the versions from parsing a whole number (§5.3). The two strings follow five rules:

1. **Text only.** Each is one text node of its own, never set through `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` (`04:965`; C-12.10), never a link and never a format string, so a `{` in a key stays a `{`. No Markdown, HTML or link is ever rendered from it, and nothing in it that looks like an address is made live (the ticket's Security notes, hazard 2; `BL:266`).
2. **Cleaned.** Before display the characters that `22`'s name rule removes — the C0 and C1 controls, U+2028, U+2029, the bidi marks and overrides U+061C, U+200E, U+200F, U+202A–U+202E and U+2066–U+2069 — each shows as a visible "�" instead of acting, as in the preview's table (§4.4), so neither string can reorder the sentence around it or hide part of itself (`22:19626–19631`); PROPOSED for a key and a file name, which no contract cleans.
3. **Capped.** A key is shown at most 64 <!-- audit:design.key_cap=64 --> characters, a file name at most 40 <!-- audit:design.file_cap=40 -->, counted after cleaning, an ellipsis (…) past the cap (the Fixture's display caps, PROPOSED). No contract bounds either string, so the cap also holds in the accessible name, which carries the same shortened text with the word "(shortened)" after it, PROPOSED: nothing past a cap is ever rendered, announced, copied or logged. The bounded strings — names, symbols, panel kinds, drawing labels — never reach a refusal, and one over its contract's length fails the schema step before any preview (§5.4). The frames' caps clause reads "the whole capped value in the accessible name": nothing past a cap reaches it.
4. **Never logged.** `layout.import_rejected{reason}` carries the reason word only, never a key, a name, a size or a line; workspace and layout content is never logged (`21:5069`) and E15-S07 logs no file contents (`BL:1136`).
5. **Nothing kept.** When a refusal shows, the worker's text and document and the file's handle are dropped: nothing reaches the workspace store, the browser's storage, a cache or a log, and Choose another file starts from nothing (BQ-5 RULED). The `state:` clause of every frame in §5.1 says it in the user's terms: no workspace, layout or pane was created and the user's workspaces are unchanged.

**The browser's checks are a courtesy** (Condition 1; `04:54`; C-2.12). The server checks size, depth, nodes, schema and version again when the file is sent (E15-S07), and `SCR-026/validating` says so in its `server:` clause; a file the browser passed can still be refused at the send, and a client that skips the checks gains nothing.

**The refusals at the send** are refusals at the point of action, T4 in E09-D09's rule, step 2 (`E09D09:32`), never a lock:

- **`SCR-026/refused-limit`** — `409 limit_reached` when another window created a workspace between the preview and the send, so the user now has 32 <!-- audit:contract.workspaces_max=32 --> workspaces (`22:8525`; `FL:164`). The message is "Nothing was imported." then E15-D02's "You have 32 workspaces, the most you can have. Delete one, then import again." (`D02:518`). The footer is Cancel alone, and it takes focus: another file would meet the same limit, and the way on is in the words. Once the dialog closes, the session's legs and states are read again and Import… reads `workspace.limit.count` in its menus (`SCR-026/entry-limit`, §6).
- **`SCR-026/refused-server`** — `422 validation_failed` when the server's own re-check refuses what the browser passed (`22:19626`): "Nothing was imported." then "The server didn't accept this file: `{key}` isn't allowed.", the key the first field path of the problem's `errors[]`, cleaned and capped as above; the server's own sentence is never shown (`15:306`). Choose another file takes focus. A refusal that names no field — the store unavailable, `503` — takes the same frame with the PROPOSED `e15.import.refused.store`, "The workspace store didn't answer. Choose the file again in a moment.", in place of the key's sentence: nothing of the file was kept, so the way on is to choose it again (§9; the problem types are D09-G7's).
- **A `403` at the send is the session's, not the file's**: view-as began or the permission went while the dialog was open. Nothing is imported, and the dialog shows `SCR-026/import-leg-lost` (§3), its verb in place with its reason (§6).
- **No event fires at the send.** The ticket's six reasons name none of these refusals; E15-S07's `semantic` would hold the server's (`BL:1136`), and D09-G13 maps the two lists.

**Never a partial import.** The send is one call that creates the workspace with all its layouts or creates nothing (D09-G1; E15-S07 lands it in `22` first, C-6.1). The browser-side sequence of `createWorkspace` then `createLayout` for each layout (`FL:171`) is not built: a failure between two calls would leave a workspace the user never chose, the partial import the ticket forbids (`11:2871`; `BL:266`). The import is built on the one-call operation, never before it, and every refusal in this section therefore leaves the user's workspaces exactly as they were.

## 6. T2 — stated once

**T2 — disabled with a visible reason, one rule for this spec (A1; BQ-14 RULED; Condition 2; every T2 row of §3 cites "§6").** E09-D09's decision rule decides every control here, the first "yes" winning (`E09D09:32`; `14:85`): a server refusal is T4 at the point of action; then the session's legs, in the server's own order, then a state the client knows. **Import… is a write** — `createWorkspace` · `workspaces:write`, scope `self` (`22:8521`; `04:1139`) — so its legs run `rbac.view_as` → `rbac.permission` → `workspace.limit.count`: first, while `GET /auth/session/view-as` reports a context, it is T2 with `rbac.view_as`, "Read-only while viewing as {user}." (`E09D09:95`), because the server refuses a view-as write before it authorises; otherwise, without `workspaces:write`, it is T2 with `rbac.permission` and the verb of `E09D09:114`, "Your role can't save workspaces." (`E09D09:89`); otherwise, at 32 <!-- audit:contract.workspaces_max=32 --> workspaces (`22:8525`), it is T2 with the state key `workspace.limit.count`, "You have the most workspaces you can have." (`D02:674`). These are the keys merged E15-D02 gives "+" › Import… (`D02:639`; `D02:655`) and merged E15-D03 renders as its R-5 (`D03:564`; its deck `D03:634–636`); this spec adds none. A workspace write is scope `self`, so the account and environment legs never apply. Each reason is one sentence of at most 60 <!-- audit:contract.reason_max=60 --> characters with a full stop that names a capability, never data (`E09D09:85–95`); the first missing leg is the one named. **The anatomy** (`E09D09:49`; `D02:641`): A T2 control keeps its native role and its full label; it is `aria-disabled="true"`, never `disabled`, so it stays in the Tab order and a menu item stays arrow-reachable; the CMP-019 `lock` glyph (`decorative=false`, named "Not available") leads the label; CMP-079 RbacGate wraps it and its reason node is in the DOM at mount, linked by `aria-describedby`. The label and the lock are `color.text.tertiary`, the reason `color.text.secondary`, a filled button's fill `color.surface.raised` with the `color.border.default` hairline — never `color.text.disabled`, never `opacity.disabled` (§8 rows 2, 5, 23 and 25). **The rendering** is 2a: in the menus, the reason as Import…'s second line; in the dialog, under Import as new workspace when a leg goes while the dialog is open (`SCR-026/import-leg-lost`). While viewing as, the SCR-124 banner is the one notice and the menu item points at it, repeating no reason (`D02:642`). **The keys** (`D02:643`): `Enter` or `Space` on a T2 control changes nothing and sends nothing; the reason is re-shown in place and announced once, politely; an arrow lands on a T2 menu item without activating it. A T2 control emits no analytics. A leg that goes while the dialog is open turns the dialog's write T2 where it stands — no reload, no focus move, the checked file and its preview kept.

### 6.1 The gated set, per session

The session's legs decide, never a role's name (`E09D09:32`): an Owner holds `workspaces:write`, a Manager holds it for their own workspaces and a Viewer does not (`04:1139`). `04:1180` marks a Viewer ✔(own) on that permission, a contradiction listed in §12 that gates nothing here, because the server enforces (C-12.4) and E15-D01's locked list says a Viewer cannot save a workspace (`D02:84`). The view-as column holds for any target.

| Control (frames)                                                                                                                           | Operation · permission                                           | Owner                                                    | Manager             | Viewer                                                                                                | View-as (any target)                                                   | At 32 <!-- audit:contract.workspaces_max=32 --> workspaces (a state)             | Rendering                                           |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| Import… in "+" and the rail's workspace menu (`SCR-026/entry`, `SCR-026/entry-view-as`, `SCR-026/entry-permission`, `SCR-026/entry-limit`) | `createWorkspace` · `workspaces:write`, scope `self` (`22:8521`) | T1                                                       | T1 (own workspaces) | T2 `rbac.permission` (`SCR-026/entry-permission`)                                                     | T2 `rbac.view_as`, the banner the one notice (`SCR-026/entry-view-as`) | T2 `workspace.limit.count` (`SCR-026/entry-limit`)                               | 2a, the item's second line; view-as: the one notice |
| Import as new workspace, the dialog's primary (`SCR-026/preview`, `SCR-026/import-leg-lost`)                                               | the one-call import, `workspaces:write` (D09-G1)                 | T1; T2 in place when a leg goes while the dialog is open | as the Owner        | never reached: Import… is T2                                                                          | never reached                                                          | a race at the send is T4 (`SCR-026/refused-limit`), then the entry reads the key | 2a under the button                                 |
| Export… in SCR-025's Share section, the tab's `[⋯]` and the rail's workspace menu; Download file (`SCR-025/*`)                             | `exportWorkspace` · `workspaces:read` (`22:12676`)               | T1                                                       | T1                  | T1 on a stored workspace; on the unsaved default an availability state (`SCR-025/export-unavailable`) | T1: a read                                                             | none                                                                             | — : never gated                                     |
| Choose file…, a drop, the five checks, the preview, Copy report                                                                            | none: the browser and its worker                                 | T1                                                       | T1                  | never reached                                                                                         | never reached                                                          | none                                                                             | — : never gated                                     |
| Cancel, `Esc`, ×, Close, Choose another file (every frame)                                                                                 | none                                                             | T1                                                       | T1                  | T1                                                                                                    | T1                                                                     | T1                                                                               | never gated (§7)                                    |

**Not T2 — each owned elsewhere, never drawn with a lock** (`D02:645`; `S4:194`):

- **Export…, a read.** No leg gates it: T1 for every session on a stored workspace, view-as included, its preview, its Download file and its completion alike (`22:12676`; `D03:384`). On the viewer's unsaved default it is an availability state — `aria-disabled`, the line "This workspace isn't saved." (`ws.export.unstored`) under it, no lock, no CMP-079, no key (`D03:583`).
- **A rejected file.** The six rejections and the two refusals at the send are outcomes (§5): Import… stays T1, since it opened the dialog, and the dialog's import verb is absent, omitted rather than T2-locked (AC 2; BQ-11 RULED).
- **An absence.** A control that is not drawn is no lock: the verb on a refusal, and Choose another file on `SCR-026/refused-limit`. The drop zone likewise, which is never a Tab stop and never needed one (§7). None of them is dimmed or carries a reason.
- **Validity.** A file that fails a check is an outcome, not a validity look, and no field here is typed, so no control is natively disabled for validity; the budget's Import anyway is a confirm, not a gate (BQ-6 RULED). Validity is never a lock.
- **Busy.** "Preparing the file…", "Checking scalping.json… step 3 of 5" and Import as new workspace in flight are `aria-busy` with their labels kept, focusable, no lock and no reason (`SCR-025/export-preparing`, `SCR-026/validating`, `SCR-026/creating`).
- **Outcomes.** The completion line, the report and "Nothing was imported." are lines after the act, never a control's state.
- **A refusal at the point of action, T4.** `409 limit_reached` and `422 validation_failed` at the send (§5.5), after which the session's legs and states are read again; a `403` there turns the dialog's verb T2 in place (`SCR-026/import-leg-lost`).
- **The exits.** Cancel, `Esc`, ×, Close and Choose another file are T1 for every session, view-as and the Viewer's included (§7).

## 7. Exits, keyboard, focus and live regions

**The exits, stated once (A3; Condition 4).** Cancel, `Esc`, ×, Close and Choose another file are never gated: never T2, never locked by view-as, a role, a limit or any state, on every frame of this spec. Before the send, Cancel, `Esc`, × and a press on the scrim close the dialog with nothing imported (`SCR-026/cancelled`; `FL:162`) or nothing exported (`FL:146`); once the import is sent, Close and `Esc` close the view and cancel nothing, the import finishing on its own (`15:468`; `SCR-026/creating`). `Esc` is reserved and never remapped (`05:85`), and it closes the topmost layer only — the OS file picker, then a menu, then the stacked confirm, then the dialog (`S4:119`; `S4:734`). A press on the scrim is Cancel on the export and the import dialog, each a `dialog`, and changes nothing on the stacked confirm, an `alertdialog` (`15:468`). No dialog here asks before it closes: a chosen file is no unsaved change, so the prompt of slice 4's H36 never appears (`S4:904`). The tool reads every frame row for it (A2): each `exits T1:` clause names its exits; no gated word sits in a clause beside an exit; and every rejection lists Choose another file among its exits.

**Each dialog's keyboard model** (`05:96`; `S4:119`; `15:463–471`). The trap cycles in DOM order and wraps; text — the trust note, a list, a message, a caption — is read, never a stop. The close × sits first in the DOM, in the title row.

**Keys.** `Tab` and `Shift+Tab` cycle inside the topmost layer only, and the page under the scrim takes no input, while the TopBar, the StatusBar and the shell's live regions stay outside it (`15:468`). `Enter` and `Space` on Choose file… or Choose another file open the OS file picker, which is the platform's own layer: a chosen file returns focus to the button that opened the picker and starts the checks, and the picker's own `Esc` or Cancel returns focus there with nothing changed (`05:101`; `FL:169`). The drop zone only adds to the input: it is never a Tab stop, has no role and no name, and every drop has the button as its keyboard equal (`05:35`; `15:694`). The preview's and the report's tables are CMP-049's table mode: one stop, the scroll region named by the caption, no control in a body row (`15:530`); a screen reader's table commands read each cell with its column header, and each Item cell is its row's header (`scope="row"`, PROPOSED: CMP-049 leaves row headers to the host), so a row reads as its item, its type, its action and its reason. On a T2 control, `Enter` and `Space` change nothing (§6).

**Announcements** (`05:144`; `05:150`; `S4:140`). Polite, and once. A dialog speaks when focus enters it; every other message goes through a region mounted empty with its host, its text inserted when the state begins, so it is never destroyed and re-created (`05:150`). No step of the checks, no tick of the progress and no drag is announced.

**200 % zoom and text size** (`05:131`; `S4:209`; `14:635`). At 200 % zoom on the 1280 × 800 frame each dialog fills the viewport less its margins, keeps its title row and its footer and scrolls its body (`SCR-025/export-reflow-200`, `SCR-026/reflow-200`); the two export lists stack in one column, and the import's tables read one row element per record (`15:529`). A footer whose labels do not fit one row wraps to two in its order, Cancel first, and never truncates a label; the stacked confirm caps the same way. The in-app text scale reflows the same. Nothing is clipped: a message wraps, and a key or a file name keeps its cap and its ellipsis (§5.5).

**Motion** is E15-D14's (out of scope). These dialogs use CMP-043's own — the scrim fades in while the card appears at once (`15:463–471`) — and reduced motion is E15-D14's to state.

## 8. Tokens, contrast, greyscale and CVD

**The token map and the redlines** (tokens by name, never a raw value; the boards bind exactly these, §0).
