**The thin margins, stated.** Every gated pair clears its bar in every theme. In light the glyphs sit nearest their non-text bar — the Add glyph on both rows (rows 6 and 9) and the success line's edge and glyph (row 17) — and none carries a meaning alone: each sits beside its word in `color.text.primary` (rows 1 and 16). Row 23 is the grey a T2 label must never take, measured to show why (`E09D09:49`); row 24 is the drop zone's resting edge, decorative, because the zone is not the control: Choose file… is, and its label and its focus ring are rows 19 and 20. Row 25 is the dialog's T2 verb on its raised fill (`SCR-026/import-leg-lost`): the menus' T2 label of row 5 on the fill E09-D09 gives a T2 button.

**Why ten are PROPOSED.** Rows 4, 6, 9, 10, 11, 13, 16, 17, 19 and 22 are pairs `tools/contrast/pairs.mjs` does not hold: the header fill, the Add glyph on both rows, the Drop and Remap glyphs on even rows, the trust note's edge, the success line's words and edge, the `secondary` label and the drag-over edge. D09-A10 asks the registry's owner to register them, and to add the Add / Drop / Remap encoding to `tools/contrast/encodings.json` with its non-colour channel, as `16:456` requires of a new status encoding; this spec edits nothing under `tools/`.

**Greyscale and colour vision** (the tool's section 3, `--json` `grey` and `cvd`; reported, never a gate on the design, while the tool checks every value below against its own; boards 03, 04 and 06 carry the greyscale and deuteranopia strips, §0). Rec. 601 luma, 0–255:

| Token                         | dark | light | hc  | What it marks                                                   |
| ----------------------------- | ---- | ----- | --- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `color.status.success.strong` | 172  | 104   | 172 | the Add glyph; the completion and report lines' edge            |
| `color.status.danger.strong`  | 184  | 108   | 184 | the Drop glyph; the rejection's glyph                           |
| `color.status.info.strong`    | 181  | 92    | 181 | the Remap glyph; the trust note's and the migration line's edge |
| `color.status.warning.strong` | 202  | 108   | 202 | the budget line's edge and glyph                                |

| Pair (ΔE76 for normal vision, protanopia, deuteranopia and tritanopia; the verdict is the tool's)             | ΔE76                       | Verdict                                     |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| the Add glyph against the Drop glyph: `color.status.success.strong` vs `color.status.danger.strong`           | 68.4 · 27.1 · 43.1 · 129.7 | distinct                                    |
| the Drop glyph against the Remap glyph: `color.status.danger.strong` vs `color.status.info.strong`            | 91.2 · 78.4 · 93.6 · 130.9 | distinct                                    |
| the Add glyph against the Remap glyph: `color.status.success.strong` vs `color.status.info.strong`            | 54.0 · 52.0 · 50.8 · 1.3   | collapses — the word and the glyph carry it |
| the Drop glyph against the budget line's glyph: `color.status.danger.strong` vs `color.status.warning.strong` | 14.7 · 12.5 · 14.0 · 5.5   | distinct                                    |

**What carries each meaning, so nothing rests on a hue** (the ticket's test plan item 5; DoD-4; `05:130`). Add, Drop and Remap are their words in their own column, each after a glyph of its own shape — a check, a circled cross, a merge — and never a row's colour (BQ-10 RULED; `14:632`). The tool's colour-vision rows show why the words must carry it: the Add and Remap glyphs collapse under tritanopia, and in light `color.status.danger.strong` and `color.status.warning.strong` are one amber, so the Drop glyph and the budget line's glyph differ by shape and words only. A rejection is its words, its glyph and the absent import verb; the budget line is its words and its numbers; T2 is the lock and the reason (§6); the drag-over state is its changed words and its edge, never the fill alone.

**Forced colours** (`S4:202`). Under `forced-colors: active` each dialog keeps its edge as a real border, which forced colours paint CanvasText; the scrim keeps its black at its opacity (`forced-color-adjust: none`); text takes CanvasText and every glyph `currentColor`, so Add, Drop and Remap stay words with their shapes; the callouts' fills fall away and their edges become CanvasText, the glyph and the words carrying the tone; the drop zone's edge stays a border and its drag-over words change as before; the focus ring becomes a Highlight outline. No meaning here is lost with the colours.

## 9. Copy deck

Every string the export and import surfaces show, keyed in `e15.export.*` and `e15.import.*`, with the registered reasons (`rbac.*`, `workspace.limit.count`, `ws.export.unstored`) and the import's cost confirm (`layout.cost.import.*`) beside them. Every frame row's copy key is a row here, and each of the tool's templates opens its String cell byte for byte, its fixture rendering after it (the tool checks both). A reason is one sentence of at most 60 <!-- audit:contract.reason_max=60 --> characters with a full stop (`E09D09:85–95`). No string names a contract field, a status code or a part of the system: the file's version is its "format version" to the user, never the field's name.

**Variables.** `{workspace}` a workspace's name (fixture Scalping); `{clash}` the name the server makes unique, Scalping (2) (`D02:516`); `{file}` a file's name — the exported file, `<slug>.cv-workspace-v<schema_version>.json` (BQ-8 RULED; D09-G14), or the chosen one, scalping.json — except in the two version refusals, where it is the file's format version, a parsed whole number (§5.3); `{app}` the format version this app reads, 1 <!-- audit:fixture.app_format=1 -->, and `{floor}` the oldest it still reads, today the same (§5.3); `{from}` and `{to}` a migration's two versions; `{i}` and `{n}` the step under way and the five steps; `{size}` the chosen file's size in MB to one decimal; `{size_mb}` and `{max}` a limit from the tool (§5.4), and `{n}` in a variant the count found in the file; `{line}` the line where the text stops being JSON; `{key}` a key path in mono, at most 64 <!-- audit:design.key_cap=64 --> characters (§5.5); `{name}` the chosen file's name, at most 40 <!-- audit:design.file_cap=40 --> characters; `{panels}` and `{layouts}` a count with its noun, singular for 1; `{dropped}` and `{remapped}` counts; `{layout}` the layout that opens (Wall); `{measured}` and `{estimate}` the frame time measured now and estimated, in ms; `{budget}` the budget, 16 <!-- audit:contract.budget_ms=16 --> ms p95 (C-14.2); `{fps}`, `{estfps}` and `{budgetfps}` each the lesser of 60 <!-- audit:contract.refresh_fps=60 --> and 1000 divided by its ms, rounded (`D03:630`); `{user}` the view-as target (alex); `{verb}` the permission's verb. Strings from the file enter only through `{key}` and `{name}`, as text (§5.5).

## 10. Design-system A-rows (`15`, PROPOSED)

This PR edits no `15` entry (BQ-1 RULED). Each row is copy-ready for the catalogue's owner: the old text is verbatim at the base, a fragment of the line named and unique in it, and the new text replaces exactly that fragment, the rest of the line unchanged. Text inside a double-backtick span is the catalogue's own source, its single backticks included; in the tables `\|` stands for `|`. The ids run D09-A1 to D09-A11 here and go on in §11; §12's hand-offs name each row's owner.

| Id      | `15` entry · field (line)                                                              | Old (verbatim)                                                                                                                                                  | New (PROPOSED)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Basis                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| D09-A1  | CMP-067 · Purpose (`15:692`); Props (`15:693`)                                         | `**Purpose**: CSV/JSON import (journal import, rule import/export).` · ``- **Props**: `accept` · `onFiles` · `multiple?`.``                                     | `**Purpose**: CSV/JSON import (journal import, rule import/export, workspace import).` · ``- **Props**: `accept` · `onFiles` · `multiple?` · `label` (the visible text of the real `<button>` that fronts the visually hidden native input and opens its picker, e.g. "Choose file…"; the input is labelled by it) · `dropHint?` (the drop zone's words, the button's `aria-describedby`, e.g. "or drop a workspace file here") · `help?` (the line under it, in the same `aria-describedby` after the drop words) · `maxBytes?` (a picked or dropped file over it is refused from its size before a byte of it is read; the host shows its error) · `error?` (the host's message node, first in the button's `aria-describedby`; the input stays live).`` | SCR-026's picker and drop zone (`SCR-026/pick`; BQ-9, BQ-13 RULED; `D02:513`); the drop target described the same way on SCR-089 (`E36D03:339`); `14:632`                                            |
| D09-A2  | CMP-067 · A11y (`15:694`)                                                              | ``- **A11y**: native `<input type=file>` under the hood (drag-and-drop is additive, never the only path), fully keyboard-operable via the native file picker.`` | ``- **A11y**: native `<input type=file>` under the hood (drag-and-drop is additive, never the only path), fully keyboard-operable via the native file picker: a real `<button>` showing its `label` opens it on `Enter` or `Space`, and a chosen file returns focus to that button; the drop zone has no role, no name and no Tab stop of its own, and a drop only adds to the input; a drop of more files than `multiple` allows, or of a type outside `accept`, is refused in words ("Drop one .json file"), never by colour alone; the host's error is first in the button's `aria-describedby` and announced once, politely.``                                                                                                                         | `05:35`; `05:101`; `14:632`; `SCR-026/drag-over-rejected`; BQ-13 RULED                                                                                                                               |
| D09-A3  | CMP-067 · Tokens (`15:695`)                                                            | ``- **Tokens**: `color.border.dashed`.``                                                                                                                        | ``- **Tokens**: the zone's edge `color.border.default` at `border.hairline`, solid, `radius.card`, padding `space.inset.xl`, its words `type.ui.body` + `color.text.secondary`; drag-over: the edge `color.border.selected` and the fill `color.surface.hover`, the words changed; the button a CMP-001 `secondary`. (Replaces `color.border.dashed`, which is not a shipped token; a dashed edge is kept for estimated and absent data.)``                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | the re-bind E36-D03 reported for the same entry (`E36D03:272`); §8 rows 22 and 24                                                                                                                    |
| D09-A4  | CMP-067 · Storybook (`15:696`); Tests (`15:697`)                                       | `- **Storybook**: Default, DragOver, Error.` · `- **Tests**: unit, visual, axe, keyboard-only file selection test.`                                             | `- **Storybook**: Default, Focused, WithHelp, DragOver, DragOverRejected, Validating, Error, DisabledWithReason, Reflow200.` · ``- **Tests**: unit, visual, axe, keyboard-only file selection test, the drop zone never in the Tab order, a drop refused by type or by count in words, a file over `maxBytes` refused before it is read.``                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | the ticket's DoD, CMP-067's Storybook list agreed with FE (`BL:266`; the owner's agreement stands in, #2701); DL-14; DisabledWithReason is a host's T2 on the button, which SCR-026 never needs (§6) |

**No A-row, and why.** CMP-001 Button: its `loading` keeps the label with `aria-busy`, and its T2 is E09-D09's (`15:48–57`). CMP-019 Icon: `x-circle` is already PROPOSED for E05-T06 (`15:307`) and `merge` by E11's icon batch, and this spec adds no glyph. CMP-023 Progress Bar: determinate, `valueText`, never a live region, as catalogued (`15:262–270`). CMP-043 Dialog: `lg` and `md` fit (`15:466`), and its focus, `Esc` and scrim rules hold as catalogued (`15:468`). CMP-045 Toast: the report is never a toast; the one toast here, after a closed view, is persistent because it carries an action, as catalogued (`15:483–490`). CMP-068 CopyableCodeBlock: unused, because no frame shows the file's text and Condition 1 forbids echoing it. CMP-079 RbacGate: T2 as catalogued (`15:811–819`). CMP-234 ValidationPanel: unused, because its entries anchor to rule rows and nodes (`15:2738–2742`) while a refusal here names one key in one message and the report is a table; E15-S07 names it for the report (`BL:1136`), a C-row (D09-C10).

## 11. `14` A-rows and the pointer lines

**Applied by this PR, content only (BQ-1 RULED).** Two pointer lines, each right after its screen's sign-off checklist — after SCR-025's (`14:624`) and after SCR-026's (`14:636`) — each starting `- **Design (hi-fi):** [E15-D09.md](../design/E15/E15-D09.md)`: SCR-025's points at §2, the export; SCR-026's at §3 to §5, the import. The tool checks on every run that each sits in the run of pointer lines right after its checklist, so a later E15-D08 pointer may sit beside ours. Nothing else in `14` is edited: both checklists stay verbatim, and §13 maps every item, the version-mismatch item to its refusal frame.

**Copy-ready for the catalogue's owner, PROPOSED** (old text verbatim at the base, a whole line or a fragment of it as named; a fragment in double quotes is the catalogue's own words; in the tables `\|` stands for `|`). Where E15-D02 already proposed the same line's change — its A-10 on SCR-026's Type, Validation and checklist (`D02:823`), pending — the row below is the one text that does both, so the owner applies one, not two.

**The reuse note for SCR-089** (DL-21; DoD-10; the ticket's Out of scope). SCR-089, rule import and export (`14:1984`), is a sibling surface that E36-D03 already draws, with CMP-067's native input first and its drop target described by its words (`E36D03:153–155`; `E36D03:339`), so D09-A19 points at this spec and changes nothing drawn there. Five things carry over as they stand: the real button with its visible label in front of the native input; the drop zone that only adds to it and is never a Tab stop; the checks in a fixed, cheapest-first order, with the server's own check stated on the checking step; the refusal that names its key in mono, capped, and never offers to bring in part of a file; every string from the file shown as text only, cleaned and capped (§5.5). Two do not: SCR-089 accepts or skips each rule on its own (`14:1993`) where a workspace is whole or nothing, and a rule bundle's version and account references follow E36's contract, not `WorkspaceBundle`'s.

## 12. A, C and G rows, and the hand-offs

Every row is PROPOSED to its owner, and none is applied by this PR: a contract changes contract-first in its owner's PR (C-6.1), `14` and `15` through the A-rows of §10 and §11, and a ruling stays the orchestrator's (6396085915) or the owner's (#2701). E15-S07 receives D09-G1 to D09-G8, D09-G13 and D09-G14 by the hand-off row below (BQ-3 RULED).

## 14. Heuristic evaluation and the owner walkthrough

**Heuristic evaluation `(heuristic)`** — Nielsen's ten and `05`'s rules, a desk review of this text and E15-D02's boards: no participant, timing or quote exists, and none is invented.

| Heuristic                                      | What the design does                                                                                                                                                              | Risk left `(heuristic)`                                                                                                                                  |
| ---------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| visibility of system status                    | the determinate progress with its step count; Import as new workspace busy, then "Still importing…"; every outcome spoken once; the report persists until the user leaves it      | a check that ends inside the 150 ms show-delay shows no bar at all, by intent; a slower one speaks once at its start and once at its end, never per step |
| match with the real world                      | "format version" and never the field's name; Add, Drop and Remap; panels, layouts and symbols, never the file's keys, except the one key a refusal names                          | a key such as `layouts[1].grid_spec.root` is the file's own language, so the sentence around it says what to do next                                     |
| user control and freedom                       | Cancel, `Esc`, × and Close are never gated (§7); a refusal keeps the dialog open with Choose another file focused; the budget's Cancel returns to the preview                     | once the file is sent, Close ends the view and the import still finishes; the toasts after a closed view carry the outcome (D09-D18)                     |
| consistency and standards                      | the trust note on every import frame; CMP-043's footer with Cancel first; one table for the preview and the report; E15-D03's T2 anatomy                                          | E15-D02's own words differ in three places until its next revision (D09-C5, D09-C6, D09-C13)                                                             |
| error prevention                               | the preview before the send; nothing created before Import as new workspace; the budget's confirm with its Cancel focused; the export's check declines what its lists cannot show | the account setting's Drop row predicts what the server does until `22` says it (D09-G3)                                                                 |
| recognition rather than recall                 | the Excluded list stays on the completion step; the report repeats the preview row for row; the file line names the chosen file                                                   | once the dialog is gone, so is the report; Copy report is the way to keep it                                                                             |
| flexibility and efficiency                     | a drop or Choose file…; `Enter` on Choose another file reopens the picker at once                                                                                                 | no chord opens Import… directly; the two menus reach it                                                                                                  |
| aesthetic and minimalist design                | one chrome for five steps; at most one CMP-028 line above the table                                                                                                               | a file with many layouts makes a long table, so its caption's counts come first                                                                          |
| recognise, diagnose and recover from errors    | each refusal names its problem and its limit, says "Nothing was imported." and gives a way on; the dialog stays open                                                              | a refusal that arrives after the view closed needs the toast's Review to recover (D09-D18)                                                               |
| help and documentation                         | the help line "JSON, up to 2 MB"; the trust note; the caveat                                                                                                                      | —                                                                                                                                                        |
| `05`: focus is never silently stolen (`05:96`) | the preview arrives with focus left on Choose file…; the report's heading takes focus only after the user's own Import as new workspace                                           | a screen-reader user meets the caption through the polite region before the table itself                                                                 |
| `05`: colour is never the only signal          | Add, Drop and Remap are words with their own shapes; a rejection is its words, its glyph and the absent verb                                                                      | under tritanopia the Add and Remap glyphs collapse (§8), so the words carry them                                                                         |
| `05`: no function by drag alone (`05:35`)      | Choose file… is the keyboard path, and the drop zone only adds to it                                                                                                              | —                                                                                                                                                        |

**The protocols** (`(heuristic)` until run). **Status: not run** — no participant, count, timing or quote exists. Ready once boards 01 to 08 exist; results are appended as a dated addendum, the ticket's test plan item by item (`BL:266`).

- **T-1 — the fixture walk** (item 1): walk every state with the fixture set — the valid file, the newer and the older format version, the malformed, the oversized, the unknown kinds, the account filter and the 30-panel file — and record the frame each reaches and its words.
- **T-2 — the keyboard walk** (item 2; board 08): from "+" › Import… to the report without a pointer, file selection included; record every stop and every announcement against §7.
- **T-3 — the screen-reader pass** (item 3): the drop zone's words, the table read with its headers, the report's region; record what is heard against §7's desk pass.
- **T-4 — the copy review of the trust claims** (item 4; boards 01 and 02): the owner reads the Excluded list and the trust note cold and says what leaves the machine and what is ignored on import. **Pre-registered rule:** a reader who believes the file may go anywhere without a thought, or that an account travels in it, makes the copy a redesign item before sign-off.
- **T-5 — contrast and colour vision** (item 5): §8's rows from the tool, and the boards' greyscale and deuteranopia renders.
- **T-6 — the token audit** (item 6): the boards PR reads every shape back as token bindings and records its counts.
- **T-7 — the checklist sweep** (item 7): §13, item by item.
- **T-8 — the hand-off dry run** (item 8; boards 02 and 04): a developer who has not read the thread rebuilds the drop zone and one rejection, `SCR-026/pick` and `SCR-026/rejected-oversized`, from this spec and those boards alone; each question the spec does not answer becomes a fix before E15-D15's hand-off pack.

**Owner walkthrough** (at most ten items; posted on #363 at merge, the boards PR drawing what each names):

- [ ] Board 01: open Export… from the tab's `[⋯]`; read the Included and the Excluded lists aloud, say what leaves the machine and what does not, and confirm that the sharing claim appears nowhere.
- [ ] Board 01: download the file; read the completion line and the file name, and say why the Excluded list is still shown.
- [ ] Board 02: open Import… and find both ways in, Choose file… and the drop zone, and say which one the keyboard reaches.
- [ ] Board 03: watch the check, read the preview's caption first and then its Drop and Remap rows, and say what will be added, what will not, and why.
- [ ] Board 04: for each rejection, say what was wrong, what the limit is and that nothing was imported, and confirm that no Import verb is offered.
- [ ] Board 05: open `wall.json`; read the budget line, press Import as new workspace and read the confirm's numbers, then press Cancel and say where focus is.
- [ ] Board 06: read the report, press Copy report and paste the text; check that no account is named and that nothing of the file appears beyond its capped names.
- [ ] Board 07: as a Viewer, and then viewing as alex, open "+" and read Import…'s lock and its reason; then say why Export… is offered to every session.
- [ ] The keyboard walk on board 08: with the keyboard alone, go from "+" › Import… through Choose file…, the OS picker, the table's headers, Import as new workspace and the report to Close, and confirm that `Esc` closes only the topmost layer and that no step needs a pointer.
- [ ] Boards 09 and 10, the light twins: tell Add, Drop and Remap apart without colour, and read the trust note and the Excluded list.

## 16. Summary

- **What it is:** the text-first hi-fi of SCR-026's workspace import — one `lg` dialog, Choose file… with its drop zone, five checks in a worker with the server's re-check stated, a preview table with its drops and remaps first, six rejections and two refusals with no Import verb, and a report with Copy report — and of the export from SCR-025 and the menus: the Included and Excluded lists, the file name with its format version, the completion.
- **Ruled:** 16 <!-- audit:bq_rows=16 --> questions and 4 <!-- audit:conditions=4 --> conditions (6396085915); the trust copy, the account rule and the version rule are the owner's, approval pending (#2701).
- **Frames:** 34 <!-- audit:frames_total=34 --> — 8 <!-- audit:frames_export=8 --> on the export, 26 <!-- audit:frames_import=26 --> on the import — with 21 <!-- audit:frames_drawn=21 --> on 10 <!-- audit:boards=10 --> boards (2 <!-- audit:light_twins=2 --> light twins) and 13 <!-- audit:frames_not_drawn=13 --> bound by the text.
- **Measured:** 23 <!-- audit:pairs_rows=23 --> gated pairs, 0 <!-- audit:pairs_fail=0 --> FAIL, 0 <!-- audit:missing_tokens=0 --> tokens missing; Add, Drop and Remap are words with their own shapes, never colour alone (§8).
- **Gated:** `--check` clean; `--self-test` runs 170 <!-- audit:mutations=170 --> mutations, each caught by exactly one new FAIL; every frame's copy key is a row of the deck (§9).
- **Rows:** 14 <!-- audit:rows.G=14 --> G, 17 <!-- audit:rows.C=17 --> C, 21 <!-- audit:rows.A=21 --> A, 4 <!-- audit:rows.Q=4 --> Q and 31 <!-- audit:rows.D=31 --> decisions, every one PROPOSED to a named owner.
- **Next:** the boards PR draws the ten boards; E15-S07 takes D09-G1 to D09-G8, D09-G13 and D09-G14, with the atomic import in `22` first (C-6.1); E15-D08 stays PENDING by name; the walkthrough of §14 is posted on #363 at merge.
